```rust
pub fn enqueue_job_with_evaluation(&mut self, job: Job, handle: &EvaluationHandle) -> JsResult<()> {
    if handle.is_cancelled() {
        let reason = handle.cancellation_reason(self).unwrap_or_else(|| make_abort_error(self));
        return Err(JsError::from_opaque(reason));
    }

    Ok(())
}

pub fn run_jobs_with_evaluation(&mut self, handle: &EvaluationHandle) -> JsResult<()> {
    if handle.is_cancelled() {
        let reason = handle.cancellation_reason(self).unwrap_or_else(|| make_abort_error(self));
        return Err(JsError::from_opaque(reason));
    }
    self.push_evaluation(handle.clone());
    let result = self.job_executor().run_jobs(self);
    self.pop_evaluation();
    result
}
```

```rust
pub(crate) fn with_evaluation<T>(&mut self, handle: &EvaluationHandle, f: impl FnOnce(&mut Self) -> T) -> T {
    self.push_evaluation(handle.clone());
    let result = f(self);
    self.pop_evaluation();
    result
}
pub(crate) fn is_evaluation_cancelled(&self) -> bool {
    !self.active_evaluations.is_empty() && self.active_evaluations.iter().any(|h| h.is_cancelled())
}
pub(crate) fn evaluation_cancellation_error(&mut self) -> JsError {
    let handles = self.active_evaluations.clone();
    for h in handles {
        if h.is_cancelled() {
            if let Some(reason) = h.cancellation_reason(self) {
                return JsError::from_opaque(reason);
            }
        }
    }
    JsError::from_opaque(make_abort_error(self))
}
```

Let me write the new `SimpleJobExecutor::enqueue_job`:
```rust
fn enqueue_job(self: Rc<Self>, job: Job, context: &mut Context) {
    self.enqueue_job_inner(job, None, context);
}
fn enqueue_job_with_evaluation(self: Rc<Self>, job: Job, handle: &EvaluationHandle, context: &mut Context) {
    self.enqueue_job_inner(job, Some(handle.clone()), context);
}
fn enqueue_job_inner(&self, job: Job, handle: Option<EvaluationHandle>, context: &mut Context) {
    match job {
        Job::PromiseJob(p) => self.promise_jobs.borrow_mut().push_back((p, handle)),
        Job::AsyncJob(a) => self.async_jobs.borrow_mut().push_back((a, handle)),
        Job::TimeoutJob(t) => {
            let now = context.clock().now();
            self.timeout_jobs
                .borrow_mut()
                .entry(now + t.timeout())
                .or_default()
                .push((t, handle));
        }
        Job::GenericJob(g) => self.generic_jobs.borrow_mut().push_back((g, handle)),
    }
}
```
But `enqueue_job` signature is `self: Rc<Self>` and `enqueue_job_inner` takes `&self`. Fine.

//! Evaluation cancellation handles.
//!
//! This module implements cooperative cancellation for JavaScript evaluation through
//! parent/child [`EvaluationHandle`]s. Cancellation is first-wins: the first effective
//! cancellation on a handle (or one of its ancestors) determines the cancellation reason,
//! and later attempts cannot replace it.

use boa_gc::{Finalize, Gc, GcRefCell, Trace};

use crate::{
    Context, JsError, JsNativeError, JsValue, js_string,
};

/// A handle to a cooperative evaluation cancellation state.
///
/// Clones of a handle share the same cancellation state. Child handles created with
/// [`EvaluationHandle::child`] inherit cancellation from their ancestors, but cancelling a
/// child does not cancel its ancestors.
#[derive(Clone, Trace, Finalize)]
pub struct EvaluationHandle {
    inner: Gc<EvaluationInner>,
}

impl std::fmt::Debug for EvaluationHandle {
    fn fmt(&self, f: &mut std::fmt::Formatter<'_>) -> std::fmt::Result {
        f.debug_struct("EvaluationHandle")
            .field("cancelled", &self.is_cancelled())
            .finish()
    }
}

impl PartialEq for EvaluationHandle {
    fn eq(&self, other: &Self) -> bool {
        Gc::ptr_eq(&self.inner, &other.inner)
    }
}

impl Eq for EvaluationHandle {}

#[derive(Debug, Trace, Finalize)]
struct EvaluationInner {
    parent: Option<EvaluationHandle>,
    state: GcRefCell<EvaluationState>,
}

#[derive(Debug, Default, Trace, Finalize)]
struct EvaluationState {
    cancelled: bool,
    reason: Option<JsValue>,
}

impl EvaluationHandle {
    /// Creates a new root evaluation handle.
    pub(crate) fn new() -> Self {
        Self {
            inner: Gc::new(EvaluationInner {
                parent: None,
                state: GcRefCell::new(EvaluationState::default()),
            }),
        }
    }

    /// Creates a child evaluation handle.
    ///
    /// Cancelling this handle does not cancel its parent, but cancelling the parent
    /// cascades to this handle.
    #[must_use]
    pub fn child(&self) -> Self {
        Self {
            inner: Gc::new(EvaluationInner {
                parent: Some(self.clone()),
                state: GcRefCell::new(EvaluationState::default()),
            }),
        }
    }

    /// Cancels this evaluation handle without a custom reason.
    ///
    /// Returns `true` if this call performed the first effective cancellation on this
    /// handle or one of its ancestors, and `false` otherwise.
    #[must_use]
    pub fn cancel(&self) -> bool {
        self.cancel_with_opt_reason(None)
    }

    /// Cancels this evaluation handle with a custom reason.
    ///
    /// Returns `true` if this call performed the first effective cancellation on this
    /// handle or one of its ancestors, and `false` otherwise.
    #[must_use]
    pub fn cancel_with_reason(&self, reason: impl Into<JsValue>) -> bool {
        self.cancel_with_opt_reason(Some(reason.into()))
    }

    fn cancel_with_opt_reason(&self, reason: Option<JsValue>) -> bool {
        if self.is_cancelled() {
            return false;
        }

        let mut state = self.inner.state.borrow_mut();
        if state.cancelled {
            return false;
        }

        state.cancelled = true;
        state.reason = reason;
        true
    }

    /// Returns `true` if this handle, or any of its ancestors, has been cancelled.
    #[must_use]
    pub fn is_cancelled(&self) -> bool {
        let mut current = Some(self.clone());
        while let Some(handle) = current {
            if handle.inner.state.borrow().cancelled {
                return true;
            }
            current = handle.inner.parent.clone();
        }
        false
    }

    /// Returns the first effective cancellation reason for this handle.
    ///
    /// If this handle has its own first effective cancellation, its reason is returned.
    /// Otherwise, the inherited cancellation reason from the nearest cancelled ancestor is
    /// returned. If cancellation happened without a custom reason, an error-like value
    /// whose string contains `AbortError` is produced.
    #[must_use]
    pub fn cancellation_reason(&self, context: &mut Context) -> Option<JsValue> {
        let mut current = Some(self.clone());
        while let Some(handle) = current {
            let state = handle.inner.state.borrow();
            if state.cancelled {
                let reason = state.reason.clone();
                return Some(reason.unwrap_or_else(|| make_abort_error(context)));
            }
            current = handle.inner.parent.clone();
        }
        None
    }
}

/// Creates an `AbortError`-like value used as the default cancellation reason.
pub(crate) fn make_abort_error(context: &mut Context) -> JsValue {
    let obj = JsNativeError::error()
        .with_message("signal is aborted without reason")
        .into_opaque(context);
    obj.set(js_string!("name"), js_string!("AbortError"), false, context)
        .ok();
    obj.into()
}

/// Convenience helper to turn a cancelled handle into a `JsError`.
pub(crate) fn cancellation_error(handle: &EvaluationHandle, context: &mut Context) -> JsError {
    let reason = handle
        .cancellation_reason(context)
        .unwrap_or_else(|| make_abort_error(context));
    JsError::from_opaque(reason)
}

    #[inline]

    }

new = '''    /// Enqueues a [`Job`] on the [`JobExecutor`].
    ///
    /// If this call happens while running under an evaluation handle, the job is
    /// automatically associated with that handle.
    #[inline]
    pub fn enqueue_job(&mut self, job: Job) {
        if let Some(handle) = self.current_evaluation_handle() {
            self.job_executor().enqueue_job_with_evaluation(job, handle, self);
        } else {
            self.job_executor().enqueue_job(job, self);
        }
    }

    /// Enqueues a [`Job`] on the [`JobExecutor`], associating it with `handle`.
    ///
    /// # Errors
    ///
    /// Returns a cancellation error if `handle` is already cancelled. In that case the job
    /// is not enqueued.
    #[inline]
    pub fn enqueue_job_with_evaluation(
        &mut self,
        job: Job,
        handle: &EvaluationHandle,
    ) -> JsResult<()> {
        if handle.is_cancelled() {
            return Err(crate::context::evaluation::cancellation_error(handle, self));
        }
        self.job_executor().enqueue_job_with_evaluation(job, handle, self);
        Ok(())
    }

    /// Runs all the jobs with the provided job executor.
    #[inline]
    pub fn run_jobs(&mut self) -> JsResult<()> {
        self.job_executor().run_jobs(self)
    }

    /// Runs all the jobs with the provided job executor under an [`EvaluationHandle`].
    ///
    /// Jobs associated with cancelled handles are skipped, and jobs spawned while draining
    /// are associated with `handle`.
    ///
    /// # Errors
    ///
    /// Returns a cancellation error if `handle` is already cancelled. In that case no queued
    /// jobs are drained by this call.
    #[inline]
    pub fn run_jobs_with_evaluation(&mut self, handle: &EvaluationHandle) -> JsResult<()> {
        if handle.is_cancelled() {
            return Err(crate::context::evaluation::cancellation_error(handle, self));
        }
        self.push_evaluation(handle.clone());
        let result = self.job_executor().run_jobs(self);
        self.pop_evaluation();
        result
    }'''

    f.write(src)

impl Context {

new = '''// ==== Private API ====

impl Context {
    /// Gets the current job executor.
    pub(crate) fn job_executor(&self) -> Rc<dyn JobExecutor> {
        self.job_executor.clone()
    }

    /// Gets the innermost active evaluation handle, if any.
    pub(crate) fn current_evaluation_handle(&self) -> Option<EvaluationHandle> {
        self.active_evaluations.last().cloned()
    }

    /// Pushes an evaluation handle onto the active evaluation stack.
    pub(crate) fn push_evaluation(&mut self, handle: EvaluationHandle) {
        self.active_evaluations.push(handle);
    }

    /// Pops the innermost active evaluation handle from the active evaluation stack.
    pub(crate) fn pop_evaluation(&mut self) {
        self.active_evaluations.pop();
    }

    /// Runs `f` with `handle` pushed onto the active evaluation stack.
    pub(crate) fn with_evaluation<T>(
        &mut self,
        handle: &EvaluationHandle,
        f: impl FnOnce(&mut Self) -> T,
    ) -> T {
        self.push_evaluation(handle.clone());
        let result = f(self);
        self.pop_evaluation();
        result
    }

    /// Returns `true` if any active evaluation handle is cancelled.
    pub(crate) fn is_evaluation_cancelled(&self) -> bool {
        !self.active_evaluations.is_empty()
            && self.active_evaluations.iter().any(EvaluationHandle::is_cancelled)
    }

    /// Builds the cancellation error from the first cancelled active evaluation handle.
    pub(crate) fn evaluation_cancellation_error(&mut self) -> JsError {
        for handle in self.active_evaluations.clone() {
            if handle.is_cancelled() {
                return crate::context::evaluation::cancellation_error(&handle, self);
            }
        }
        JsError::from_opaque(crate::context::evaluation::make_abort_error(self))
    }
'''
assert old in src
src = src.replace(old, new, 1)

    f.write(src)

    /// Evaluates this script under an [`EvaluationHandle`] and returns its result.
    ///
    /// If the handle is already cancelled, this fails before any user code runs.
    ///
    /// Note that this won't run any scheduled promise jobs; you need to call [`Context::run_jobs`]
    /// on the context or [`JobExecutor::run_jobs`] on the provided queue to run them.
    ///
    /// [`JobExecutor::run_jobs`]: crate::job::JobExecutor::run_jobs
    pub fn evaluate_with_evaluation(
        &self,
        handle: &EvaluationHandle,
        context: &mut Context,
    ) -> JsResult<JsValue> {
        if handle.is_cancelled() {
            return Err(crate::context::evaluation::cancellation_error(handle, context));
        }

        self.prepare_run(context)?;

        context.push_evaluation(handle.clone());
        let record = context.run();
        context.pop_evaluation();

        context.vm.pop_frame();

        record.consume()
    }'''

Now update Module with `evaluate_with_evaluation` and `load_link_evaluate_with_evaluation`. Let me edit module/mod.rs. First add import for

Let me add the methods after `evaluate` and after `load_link_evaluate`. Let me write `evaluate_with_evaluation`:
```rust
pub fn evaluate_with_evaluation(&self, handle: &EvaluationHandle, context: &mut Context) -> JsResult<JsPromise> {
    if handle.is_cancelled() {
        let reason = handle.cancellation_reason(context).unwrap_or_else(|| make_abort_error(context));
        return JsPromise::reject(JsError::from_opaque(reason), context).map_err(Into::into);
    }
    context.with_evaluation(handle, |context| self.evaluate(context))
}
```
Wait, `self.evaluate(context)` returns `JsResult<JsPromise>`. `context.with_evaluation` takes `&EvaluationHandle` and closure `FnOnce(&mut Context) ->
T`. Here `self` is `&Module`. The closure captures `self` and takes `context`. Fine.

But there's a subtlety: `context.with_evaluation(handle, |context| self.evaluate(context))` - the inner `context` shadows outer. Fine.

For `load_link_evaluate_with_evaluation`:
```rust
pub fn load_link_evaluate_with_evaluation(&self, handle: &EvaluationHandle, context: &mut Context) -> JsPromise {
    if handle.is_cancelled() {
        let reason = handle.cancellation_reason(context).unwrap_or_else(|| make_abort_error(context));
        return JsPromise::reject(JsError::from_opaque(reason), context)
            .expect("promise rejection cannot fail when using the %Promise% intrinsic");
    }

    let module = self.clone();
    let handle_link = handle.clone();
    let handle_eval = handle.clone();

    self.load(context)
        .then(
            Some(
                NativeFunction::from_copy_closure_with_captures(
                    |_, _, (handle, module), context| {
                        if handle.is_cancelled() {
                            return Err(crate::context::evaluation::cancellation_error(&handle, context));
                        }
                        context.with_evaluation(&handle, |context| {
                            module.link(context)?;
                            Ok(JsValue::undefined())
                        })
                    },
                    (handle_link, self.clone()),
                )
                .to_js_function(context.realm()),
            ),
            None,
            context,
        )
        .expect("`then` cannot fail for a native `JsPromise`")
        .then(
            Some(
                NativeFunction::from_copy_closure_with_captures(
                    |_, _, (handle, module), context| {
                        if handle.is_cancelled() {
                            return Err(crate::context::evaluation::cancellation_error(&handle, context));
                        }
                        context.with_evaluation(&handle, |context| {
                            Ok(module.evaluate(context)?.into())
                        })
                    },
                    (handle_eval, self.clone()),
                )
                .to_js_function(context.realm()),
            ),
            None,
            context,
        )
        .expect("`then` cannot fail for a native `JsPromise`")
}
```

Wait, there's an issue: `from_copy_closure_with_captures` requires the closure to be `Copy`. `|_, _, (handle, module), context| ...` - the closure
itself doesn't capture anything (captures are passed separately as T). So it's `Copy` (a fn-like closure with no captures). Good. `T =
(EvaluationHandle, Module)` must be `Trace + 'static`. `EvaluationHandle` is Trace, `Module` is Trace. Good.

But there's a subtlety: `module.evaluate(context)` inside `with_evaluation` - the closure returns `JsResult<JsValue>`. `with_evaluation` returns `T =
JsResult<JsValue>`. So the callback returns `JsResult<JsValue>`. Good.

For the link callback: `module.link(context)?; Ok(JsValue::undefined())` returns `JsResult<JsValue>`. Good.

Now, `load_link_evaluate` current implementation uses `self.load(context).then(Some(link), None, context).expect(...).then(Some(eval), None,
context).expect(...)`. I'll mirror that.

Now, there's one more requirement consideration: For `load_link_evaluate_with_evaluation`, "must check cancellation at phase boundaries so
cancellation after load but before evaluate still rejects and prevents side effects." Our link callback checks before linking, and eval callback
checks before evaluating. If cancellation happens after load (i.e., after `load` promise resolves) but before link callback runs, the link callback
sees `handle.is_cancelled()` and returns Err (rejects), preventing link side effects. If cancellation after link but before evaluate, eval callback
rejects, preventing evaluation side effects. Good.

But wait - what about cancellation happening *during* the load phase? The load phase may enqueue async jobs. Those jobs (module loading) are spawned
during `self.load(context)` which is called... hmm, in our `load_link_evaluate_with_evaluation`, we call `self.load(context)` at the top without
`with_evaluation`. Should load run under the handle? If load spawns async jobs, requirement 10 says jobs spawned by code running under a handle are
associated. The load phase itself isn't "code running under an evaluation handle" unless we push it. But the requirement for load_link says check at
phase boundaries. To make load jobs cancellable, we might want to run `self.load(context)` under the handle too. Let me wrap the initial load call:
```rust
let load_promise = context.with_evaluation(handle, |context| self.load(context));
```
But `with_evaluation` returns `T = JsPromise`. Good. This ensures jobs spawned during load are associated with the handle. Also if the handle is
already cancelled (checked at entry), we return rejected promise before load. During load, if cancellation happens, the load jobs may be skipped (when
run), and the load promise may or may not resolve. The link callback check would catch it if cancelled before link.

Actually, there's a subtlety: if load is cancelled mid-flight, `load` may still resolve (because load completion happens in async jobs; if those jobs
are skipped due to cancellation, the load promise may never resolve, leaving the chain pending). But requirement only says cancellation after load but
before evaluate must reject. If load doesn't complete, the chain stays pending - that might be acceptable. But to be safe, we could have the load
phase also check cancellation. However, the requirement specifically says "check cancellation at phase boundaries so cancellation after load but
before evaluate still rejects". So checking at link boundary is enough. Let me wrap load under handle anyway for job association.

Now, there's another subtlety: in `load_link_evaluate` (plain), `self.load(context)` is called and then `.then(...)`. The `.then` callbacks run as
jobs later. If we wrap `self.load(context)` in `with_evaluation`, the handle is popped immediately after `load` returns (which just enqueues async
load jobs). Good.

Let me also think: should the link callback check cancellation *inside* `with_evaluation` or before? If handle is cancelled, we return Err without
running link. Good. If not cancelled, we push handle, run link. During link, cancellation could happen (if link triggers JS? link is mostly native but
may call host hooks). If link returns normally despite cancellation, then eval callback checks again. Fine.

    /// Abstract operation [`GetModuleNamespace ( module )`][spec].
    ///
    /// Gets the [**Module Namespace Object**][ns] that represents this module's exports.
    ///
    /// [spec]: https://tc39.es/ecma262/#sec-getmodulenamespace
    /// [ns]: https://tc39.es/ecma262/#sec-module-namespace-exotic-objects
    pub fn namespace(&self, context: &mut Context) -> JsObject {
        // 1. Assert: If module is a Cyclic Module Record, then module.[[Status]] is not new or unlinked.
        // 2. Let namespace be module.[[Namespace]].
        // 3. If namespace is empty, then
        // 4. Return namespace.
        self.inner
            .namespace
            .borrow_mut()
            .get_or_insert_with(|| {
                // a. Let exportedNames be module.GetExportedNames().
                let exported_names =
                    self.get_exported_names(&mut Vec::default(), context.interner());

                // b. Let unambiguousNames be a new empty List.
                let unambiguous_names = exported_names
                    .into_iter()
                    // c. For each element name of exportedNames, do
                    .filter_map(|name| {
                        // i. Let resolution be module.ResolveExport(name).
                        // ii. If resolution is a ResolvedBinding Record, append name to unambiguousNames.
                        self.resolve_export(&name, &mut HashSet::default(), context.interner())
                            .ok()
                            .map(|_| name)
                    })
                    .collect();

                //     d. Set namespace to ModuleNamespaceCreate(module, unambiguousNames).
                ModuleNamespace::create(self.clone(), unambiguous_names, context)
            })
            .clone()
    }

    /// Get an exported value from the module.
    #[inline]

    /// Abstract operation [`GetModuleNamespace ( module )`][spec].'''
new = '''            )
            .expect("`then` cannot fail for a native `JsPromise`")
    }

    /// Loads, links and evaluates this module under an [`EvaluationHandle`], returning a
    /// promise that will resolve after the module finishes its lifecycle.
    ///
    /// Cancellation is checked at each phase boundary: if the handle is cancelled before
    /// linking or before evaluating, the returned promise is rejected with the cancellation
    /// reason and the corresponding phase is skipped.
    #[allow(clippy::redundant_closure)]
    #[inline]
    pub fn load_link_evaluate_with_evaluation(
        &self,
        handle: &EvaluationHandle,
        context: &mut Context,
    ) -> JsPromise {
        if handle.is_cancelled() {
            let reason = handle
                .cancellation_reason(context)
                .unwrap_or_else(|| crate::context::evaluation::make_abort_error(context));
            return JsPromise::reject(JsError::from_opaque(reason), context)
                .expect("promise rejection cannot fail when using the `%Promise%` intrinsic");
        }

        let load_promise = context.with_evaluation(handle, |context| self.load(context));

        let handle_link = handle.clone();
        let handle_eval = handle.clone();

        load_promise
            .then(
                Some(
                    NativeFunction::from_copy_closure_with_captures(
                        |_, _, (handle, module), context| {
                            if handle.is_cancelled() {
                                return Err(crate::context::evaluation::cancellation_error(
                                    &handle, context,
                                ));
                            }
                            context.with_evaluation(&handle, |context| {
                                module.link(context)?;
                                Ok(JsValue::undefined())
                            })
                        },
                        (handle_link, self.clone()),
                    )
                    .to_js_function(context.realm()),
                ),
                None,
                context,
            )
            .expect("`then` cannot fail for a native `JsPromise`")
            .then(
                Some(
                    NativeFunction::from_copy_closure_with_captures(
                        |_, _, (handle, module), context| {
                            if handle.is_cancelled() {
                                return Err(crate::context::evaluation::cancellation_error(
                                    &handle, context,
                                ));
                            }
                            context.with_evaluation(&handle, |context| {
                                Ok(module.evaluate(context)?.into())
                            })
                        },
                        (handle_eval, self.clone()),
                    )
                    .to_js_function(context.realm()),
                ),
                None,
                context,
            )
            .expect("`then` cannot fail for a native `JsPromise`")
    }

    /// Abstract operation [`GetModuleNamespace ( module )`][spec].'''

    /// that the job can be skipped if the handle is cancelled before it starts. The default

    fn enqueue_job_with_evaluation(
        self: Rc<Self>,
        job: Job,
        handle: &EvaluationHandle,
        context: &mut Context,

        self.enqueue_job(job, context);

impl JobExecutor for SimpleJobExecutor {
    fn enqueue_job(self: Rc<Self>, job: Job, context: &mut Context) {
        match job {
            Job::PromiseJob(p) => self.promise_jobs.borrow_mut().push_back(p),
            Job::AsyncJob(a) => self.async_jobs.borrow_mut().push_back(a),
            Job::TimeoutJob(t) => {
                let now = context.clock().now();
                self.timeout_jobs
                    .borrow_mut()
                    .entry(now + t.timeout())
                    .or_default()
                    .push(t);
            }
            Job::GenericJob(g) => self.generic_jobs.borrow_mut().push_back(g),
        }
    }

    fn run_jobs(self: Rc<Self>, context: &mut Context) -> JsResult<()> {
        future::block_on(self.run_jobs_async(&RefCell::new(context)))
    }

    async fn run_jobs_async(self: Rc<Self>, context: &RefCell<&mut Context>) -> JsResult<()>
    where
        Self: Sized,
    {
        let mut group = FutureGroup::new();
        loop {
            if self.stop.load(Ordering::Relaxed) {
                self.stop.store(false, Ordering::Relaxed);
                self.clear();
                return Ok(());
            }

            for job in mem::take(&mut *self.async_jobs.borrow_mut()) {
                group.insert(job.call(context));
            }

            // Dispatch all past-due timeout jobs before the termination check.
            {
                let now = context.borrow().clock().now();
                let jobs_to_run = {
                    let mut timeout_jobs = self.timeout_jobs.borrow_mut();
                    let mut jobs_to_keep = timeout_jobs.split_off(&now);
                    jobs_to_keep.retain(|_, jobs| {
                        jobs.retain(|job| !job.is_cancelled());
                        !jobs.is_empty()
                    });
                    mem::replace(&mut *timeout_jobs, jobs_to_keep)
                };

                for jobs in jobs_to_run.into_values() {
                    for job in jobs {
                        if !job.is_cancelled()
                            && let Err(err) = job.call(&mut context.borrow_mut())
                        {
                            self.clear();
                            return Err(err);
                        }
                    }
                }
            }

            if self.is_empty() && group.is_empty() {
                break;
            }

            if let Some(Err(err)) = future::poll_once(group.next()).await.flatten() {
                self.clear();
                return Err(err);
            }

            let jobs = mem::take(&mut *self.promise_jobs.borrow_mut());
            for job in jobs {
                if let Err(err) = job.call(&mut context.borrow_mut()) {
                    self.clear();
                    return Err(err);
                }
            }

            let jobs = mem::take(&mut *self.generic_jobs.borrow_mut());
            for job in jobs {
                if let Err(err) = job.call(&mut context.borrow_mut()) {
                    self.clear();
                    return Err(err);
                }
            }
            context.borrow_mut().clear_kept_objects();
            future::yield_now().await;
        }

pub struct SimpleJobExecutor {
    promise_jobs: RefCell<VecDeque<PromiseJob>>,
    async_jobs: RefCell<VecDeque<NativeAsyncJob>>,
    timeout_jobs: RefCell<BTreeMap<JsInstant, Vec<TimeoutJob>>>,
    generic_jobs: RefCell<VecDeque<GenericJob>>,
    stop: Arc<AtomicBool>,
}

impl SimpleJobExecutor {
    fn clear(&self) {
        self.promise_jobs.borrow_mut().clear();
        self.async_jobs.borrow_mut().clear();
        self.timeout_jobs.borrow_mut().clear();
        self.generic_jobs.borrow_mut().clear();
    }
}

impl Debug for SimpleJobExecutor {
    fn fmt(&self, f: &mut std::fmt::Formatter<'_>) -> std::fmt::Result {
        f.debug_struct("SimpleJobExecutor").finish_non_exhaustive()
    }
}

impl SimpleJobExecutor {
    /// Creates a new `SimpleJobExecutor`.
    #[must_use]
    pub fn new() -> Self {
        Self::default()
    }

    /// Gets the cancellation token for this executor.
    ///
    /// Setting the signal to `true` will exit the inner event loop and
    /// stop executing any pending jobs.
    pub fn get_cancellation_token(&self) -> Arc<AtomicBool> {
        Arc::clone(&self.stop)
    }

    pub(crate) fn is_empty(&self) -> bool {
        self.promise_jobs.borrow().is_empty()
            && self.async_jobs.borrow().is_empty()
            && self.generic_jobs.borrow().is_empty()
            && self.timeout_jobs.borrow().is_empty()
    }
}

new_impl = '''impl JobExecutor for SimpleJobExecutor {
    fn enqueue_job(self: Rc<Self>, job: Job, context: &mut Context) {
        self.enqueue_job_inner(job, None, context);
    }

    fn enqueue_job_with_evaluation(
        self: Rc<Self>,
        job: Job,
        handle: &EvaluationHandle,
        context: &mut Context,
    ) {
        self.enqueue_job_inner(job, Some(handle.clone()), context);
    }

    fn run_jobs(self: Rc<Self>, context: &mut Context) -> JsResult<()> {
        future::block_on(self.run_jobs_async(&RefCell::new(context)))
    }

    async fn run_jobs_async(self: Rc<Self>, context: &RefCell<&mut Context>) -> JsResult<()>
    where
        Self: Sized,
    {
        let mut group = FutureGroup::new();
        loop {
            if self.stop.load(Ordering::Relaxed) {
                self.stop.store(false, Ordering::Relaxed);
                self.clear();
                return Ok(());
            }

            for (job, handle) in mem::take(&mut *self.async_jobs.borrow_mut()) {
                if handle.as_ref().is_some_and(EvaluationHandle::is_cancelled) {
                    continue;
                }
                group.insert(job.call(context));
            }

            // Dispatch all past-due timeout jobs before the termination check.
            {
                let now = context.borrow().clock().now();
                let jobs_to_run = {
                    let mut timeout_jobs = self.timeout_jobs.borrow_mut();
                    let mut jobs_to_keep = timeout_jobs.split_off(&now);
                    jobs_to_keep.retain(|_, jobs| {
                        jobs.retain(|(job, handle)| {
                            !job.is_cancelled()
                                && handle.as_ref().is_none_or(|h| !h.is_cancelled())
                        });
                        !jobs.is_empty()
                    });
                    mem::replace(&mut *timeout_jobs, jobs_to_keep)
                };

                for jobs in jobs_to_run.into_values() {
                    for (job, handle) in jobs {
                        if !job.is_cancelled()
                            && handle.as_ref().is_none_or(|h| !h.is_cancelled())
                            && let Err(err) = job.call(&mut context.borrow_mut())
                        {
                            self.clear();
                            return Err(err);
                        }
                    }
                }
            }

            if self.is_empty() && group.is_empty() {
                break;
            }

            if let Some(Err(err)) = future::poll_once(group.next()).await.flatten() {
                self.clear();
                return Err(err);
            }

            let jobs = mem::take(&mut *self.promise_jobs.borrow_mut());
            for (job, handle) in jobs {
                if handle.as_ref().is_none_or(|h| !h.is_cancelled())
                    && let Err(err) = job.call(&mut context.borrow_mut())
                {
                    self.clear();
                    return Err(err);
                }
            }

            let jobs = mem::take(&mut *self.generic_jobs.borrow_mut());
            for (job, handle) in jobs {
                if handle.as_ref().is_none_or(|h| !h.is_cancelled())
                    && let Err(err) = job.call(&mut context.borrow_mut())
                {
                    self.clear();
                    return Err(err);
                }
            }
            context.borrow_mut().clear_kept_objects();
            future::yield_now().await;
        }'''

        match job {
            Job::PromiseJob(p) => self.promise_jobs.borrow_mut().push_back((p, handle)),

STEP 68 SOURCE agent
REASONING_CONTENT
While that compiles, let me write the reproduction test. Let me create a test file under `core/engine/src/tests/evaluation.rs` and register it in
`mod.rs`. This will let me validate behavior.

Let me think about the test cases:
1. Basic cancel: `handle.cancel()` returns true; `is_cancelled()` true; `cancellation_reason(context)` returns AbortError-like with string containing
"AbortError".
2. `cancel_with_reason(js_string!("my reason"))` returns true; reason is Some("my reason"); later cancel returns false.
3. Child handles: parent.cancel() -> child.is_cancelled() true; child.cancel() doesn't cancel parent; child.cancellation_reason surfaces parent reason
unless child has own.
4. First-wins: child cancelled with reason R1, parent cancelled with R2; child reason stays R1; parent cancel returns true.
5. `Script::evaluate_with_evaluation` with already-cancelled handle returns Err before user code runs.
6. Cancelling during script execution stops before later side effects. E.g., register a native function `cancelFn` that calls `handle.cancel()`, then
script `globalThis.a = 1; cancelFn(); globalThis.a = 2;` should fail and `a` should be 1.
7. `Context::eval_with_evaluation` with cancelled handle fails.
8. `Module::evaluate_with_evaluation` with already-cancelled handle returns Ok(rejected promise with reason).
9. `Module::load_link_evaluate_with_evaluation` returns promise; cancelled before evaluate rejects.
10. `Context::enqueue_job_with_evaluation` fails when already cancelled and doesn't enqueue.
11. Jobs enqueued with handle are skipped when cancelled.
12. Jobs spawned under handle auto-associated.
13. `run_jobs_with_evaluation` fails immediately when already cancelled.
14. Parent cancellation cascades to queued jobs.

use std::{
    cell::{Cell, RefCell},
    pin::pin,
    rc::Rc,
};

use futures_lite::future;

use crate::{
    JsValue, TestAction,
    context::{ContextBuilder, time::FixedClock},
    job::{GenericJob, JobExecutor, NativeAsyncJob, SimpleJobExecutor},
    run_test_actions_with,
};

#[test]
fn test_async_job_not_blocking_event_loop() {
    let clock = Rc::new(FixedClock::default());
    let context = &mut ContextBuilder::default()
        .clock(clock.clone())
        .build()
        .unwrap();

    run_test_actions_with(
        [TestAction::inspect_context_async(async move |ctx| {
            let executor = ctx.downcast_job_executor::<SimpleJobExecutor>().unwrap();
            let ctx = &RefCell::new(ctx);

            let mut event_loop = pin!(future::poll_once(executor.run_jobs_async(ctx)));

            // There are no jobs in our queue. Push
            // an async job that will consistently yield to the executor.
            ctx.borrow_mut().enqueue_job(
                NativeAsyncJob::new(async |_| {
                    loop {
                        future::yield_now().await;
                    }
                })
                .into(),
            );

            // Then, start the event loop
            assert!(event_loop.as_mut().await.is_none());

            let checker = Rc::new(Cell::new(false));
            {
                let checker = checker.clone();
                // At this point, the event loop should have yielded again to the async executor.
                // Thus, enqueue a generic job that should resolve in the next loop.
                let realm = ctx.borrow().realm().clone();
                ctx.borrow_mut().enqueue_job(
                    GenericJob::new(
                        move |_| {
                            checker.set(true);
                            Ok(JsValue::undefined())
                        },
                        realm,
                    )
                    .into(),
                );
            }

            // Next iteration of the event loop
            assert!(event_loop.as_mut().await.is_none());

            // At this point, our generic job should have been executed.
            assert!(checker.get());
        })],
        context,
    );
}

```rust
use crate::{
    JsError, JsValue, Source,
    job::{GenericJob, NativeJob},
    js_string,
};

#[test]
fn basic_cancel() {
    let mut context = Context::default();
    let handle = context.new_evaluation_handle();
    assert!(!handle.is_cancelled());
    assert!(handle.cancellation_reason(&mut context).is_none());

    assert!(handle.cancel());
    assert!(handle.is_cancelled());
    assert!(!handle.cancel());
    assert!(!handle.cancel_with_reason(js_string!("second")));
    let reason = handle.cancellation_reason(&mut context).unwrap();
    let str = reason.to_string(&mut context).unwrap().to_std_string_escaped();
    assert!(str.contains("AbortError"), "{str}");
}
```

Test 3: child behavior.
```rust
#[test]
fn parent_child_cancellation() {
    let mut context = Context::default();
    let parent = context.new_evaluation_handle();
    let child = parent.child();
    let grandchild = child.child();

    // Child cancellation does not cancel parent.
    assert!(child.cancel_with_reason(js_string!("child reason")));
    assert!(child.is_cancelled());
    assert!(!parent.is_cancelled());
    assert!(!grandchild.is_cancelled()); // wait - grandchild's parent is child which is cancelled, so grandchild IS cancelled!
    // Actually child.cancel -> grandchild is_cancelled true.
```

Wait, careful: if child is cancelled, grandchild (descendant) should be cancelled (cascade). So grandchild.is_cancelled() == true. And child.cancel()
returns true because neither parent nor child was cancelled. Let me adjust:
```rust
assert!(child.cancel_with_reason(js_string!("child reason")));
assert!(child.is_cancelled());
assert!(!parent.is_cancelled());
assert!(grandchild.is_cancelled());
// child has own reason
assert_eq!(child.cancellation_reason(&mut context).unwrap(), JsValue::from(js_string!("child reason")));
// grandchild inherits child's reason
assert_eq!(grandchild.cancellation_reason(&mut context).unwrap(), JsValue::from(js_string!("child reason")));
// parent still not cancelled
assert!(parent.cancellation_reason(&mut context).is_none());

// Parent cancellation cascades.
assert!(parent.cancel_with_reason(js_string!("parent reason")));
assert!(parent.is_cancelled());
// child already has own first effective reason, so keeps it
assert_eq!(child.cancellation_reason(&mut context).unwrap(), JsValue::from(js_string!("child reason")));
// another child of parent inherits parent reason
let sibling = parent.child();
assert!(sibling.is_cancelled());
assert_eq!(sibling.cancellation_reason(&mut context).unwrap(), JsValue::from(js_string!("parent reason")));
// later cancel on child returns false (already effectively cancelled)
assert!(!child.cancel());
assert!(!sibling.cancel_with_reason(js_string!("sibling reason")));
```

Test 5: cancelling during script execution stops before later side effects.
```rust
#[test]
fn cancel_during_script_stops_side_effects() {
    let mut context = Context::default();
    let handle = context.new_evaluation_handle();
    let handle_clone = handle.clone();
    context.register_global_callable(
        js_string!("cancelFn"),
        0,
        NativeFunction::from_copy_closure_with_captures(
            move |_, _, handle, _| {
                handle.cancel();
                Ok(JsValue::undefined())
            },
            handle_clone,
        ),
    ).unwrap();
    let result = context.eval_with_evaluation(
        Source::from_bytes("globalThis.before = 1; cancelFn(); globalThis.after = 2;"),
        &handle,
    );
    assert!(result.is_err());
    assert_eq!(context.eval("globalThis.before").unwrap(), 1.into());
    assert!(context.eval("globalThis.after").unwrap().is_undefined());
}
```

    ///     )
    ///     .expect("property shouldn't exist");
    /// ```
    pub fn register_global_property<K, V>(
        &mut self,
        key: K,
        value: V,
        attribute: Attribute,
    ) -> JsResult<()>
    where
        K: Into<PropertyKey>,
        V: Into<JsValue>,
    {
        self.global_object().define_property_or_throw(
            key,
            PropertyDescriptor::builder()
                .value(value)
                .writable(attribute.writable())
                .enumerable(attribute.enumerable())
                .configurable(attribute.configurable()),
            self,
        )?;
        Ok(())
    }

    /// Register a global native callable.
    ///
    /// The function will be both `constructable` (call with `new <name>()`) and `callable` (call
    /// with `<name>()`).
    ///
    /// The function will be bound to the global object with `writable`, `non-enumerable`
    /// and `configurable` attributes. The same as when you create a function in JavaScript.
    ///
    /// # Note
    ///
    /// If you wish to only create the function object without binding it to the global object, you
    /// can use the [`FunctionObjectBuilder`] API.
    pub fn register_global_callable(
        &mut self,
        name: JsString,
        length: usize,
        body: NativeFunction,
    ) -> JsResult<()> {
        let function = FunctionObjectBuilder::new(self.realm(), body)
            .name(name.clone())
            .length(length)
            .constructor(true)
            .build();

        self.global_object().define_property_or_throw(
            name,
            PropertyDescriptor::builder()
                .value(function)
                .writable(true)
                .enumerable(false)
                .configurable(true),
            self,
        )?;
        Ok(())
    }

    /// Register a global native function that is not a constructor.
    ///
    /// The function will be bound to the global object with `writable`, `non-enumerable`
    /// and `configurable` attributes. The same as when you create a function in JavaScript.
    ///
    /// # Note
    ///
    /// The difference to [`Context::register_global_callable`] is, that the function will not be
    /// `constructable`. Usage of the function as a constructor will produce a `TypeError`.
    pub fn register_global_builtin_callable(
        &mut self,
        name: JsString,
        length: usize,
        body: NativeFunction,
    ) -> JsResult<()> {

#![allow(unused_crate_dependencies, missing_docs)]

use std::cell::RefCell;
use std::rc::Rc;

use boa_engine::builtins::promise::PromiseState;
use boa_engine::module::{ModuleLoader, Referrer};
use boa_engine::{Context, JsResult, JsString, Module, Source, js_string};

#[test]
fn test_json_module_from_str() {
    struct TestModuleLoader(JsString);
    impl ModuleLoader for TestModuleLoader {
        async fn load_imported_module(
            self: Rc<Self>,
            _referrer: Referrer,
            request: boa_engine::module::ModuleRequest,
            context: &RefCell<&mut Context>,
        ) -> JsResult<Module> {
            assert_eq!(request.specifier().to_std_string_escaped(), "basic");
            let src = self.0.clone();

            Ok(Module::parse_json(src, &mut context.borrow_mut()).unwrap())
        }
    }

    let json_string = js_string!(r#"{"key":"value","other":123}"#);
    let mut context = Context::builder()
        .module_loader(Rc::new(TestModuleLoader(json_string.clone())))
        .build()
        .unwrap();

    let source = Source::from_bytes(
        b"
        import basic_json from 'basic';
        export let json = basic_json;
    ",
    );

    let module = Module::parse(source, None, &mut context).unwrap();
    let promise = module.load_link_evaluate(&mut context);
    context.run_jobs().unwrap();

    match promise.state() {
        PromiseState::Pending => {}
        PromiseState::Fulfilled(v) => {
            assert!(v.is_undefined());
        }
        PromiseState::Rejected(e) => {
            panic!("Unexpected error: {:?}", e.to_string(&mut context).unwrap());
        }
    }

    let json = module
        .namespace(&mut context)
        .get(js_string!("json"), &mut context)
        .unwrap();

    assert_eq!(
        JsString::from(json.to_json(&mut context).unwrap().unwrap().to_string()),
        json_string
    );
}

Test: job spawned under handle is auto-associated:
```rust
#[test]
fn jobs_spawned_under_handle_are_associated() {
    let mut context = Context::default();
    let handle = context.new_evaluation_handle();
    let executed = Rc::new(Cell::new(false));
    let executed_clone = executed.clone();
    let handle2 = handle.clone();
    let realm = context.realm().clone();
    // Register a native function that enqueues a job.
    context.register_global_callable(
        js_string!("enqueueJob"),
        0,
        NativeFunction::from_copy_closure_with_captures(
            move |_, _, (handle, executed), context| {
                let job = GenericJob::new(move |_| { executed.set(true); Ok(JsValue::undefined()) }, context.realm().clone()).into();
                context.enqueue_job(job);
                handle.cancel();
                Ok(JsValue::undefined())
            },
            (handle2, executed_clone),
        ),
    ).unwrap();

    // Run a script under the handle that calls enqueueJob.
    let result = context.eval_with_evaluation(Source::from_bytes("enqueueJob();"), &handle);
    assert!(result.is_err()); // cancellation aborts script
    context.run_jobs().unwrap();
    assert!(!executed.get()); // spawned job skipped because handle cancelled
}
```
Wait, inside the closure we call `context.enqueue_job(job)` while the active handle is H (because we're running under eval_with_evaluation). So the
job is auto-associated with H. Then `handle.cancel()`. Then the script aborts. Then `run_jobs` should skip the job. Good.

But careful: `context.enqueue_job(job)` - the closure has `context: &mut Context`. It captures `handle` and `executed`. `handle.cancel()` after
enqueue. Good.

it should be skipped. Good.

Test: module evaluate with already-cancelled handle returns Ok(rejected promise).
```rust
#[test]
fn module_evaluate_with_cancelled_handle_returns_rejected_promise() {
    let mut context = Context::default();

    handle.cancel_with_reason(js_string!("module reason"));
```

Wait, but `Module::evaluate_with_evaluation` requires the module to be linked first? The spec says evaluate must only be called if link succeeded. But
if handle is already cancelled, we return before calling evaluate, so no link needed. Good.


Test: module load_link_evaluate_with_evaluation checks cancellation at phase boundaries (after load before evaluate):
This is trickier. We need to cancel after load but before evaluate. We can create a module whose load is async and completes only when we let it. Or
simpler: create a module, call `load_link_evaluate_with_evaluation`, then run jobs partially? Actually the load of a simple module happens
synchronously (no imports) and resolves immediately, but the `.then` callbacks are jobs that run during `run_jobs`. So:
1. Call `load_link_evaluate_with_evaluation(handle)` -> returns promise (pending).
2. Cancel the handle (after load started but before jobs run).
3. `context.run_jobs()` -> the link callback runs, sees handle cancelled, rejects.
4. Assert promise rejected with reason and side effects prevented (e.g., module evaluation didn't set global).

But wait - does `Module::load` enqueue the resolution as a job immediately? `load` creates a promise capability and `inner_load` resolves it
synchronously? Let me check `inner_load` - after loading, it calls `state.capability.resolve().call(...)` synchronously (not enqueued as job). Then
`.then(link_fn)` registers a reaction. When the promise is already resolved, the reaction is enqueued as a microtask (job). So after
`load_link_evaluate_with_evaluation` returns, the promise is resolved (fulfilled) but the `.then` callback is pending in the job queue. Then we
cancel. Then `run_jobs` runs the link callback which sees cancelled. Good.

But for a simple module with no imports, does `load` resolve synchronously? Let's check `inner_load`: it processes requested modules; if none,
pending_modules becomes 0 and it calls resolve synchronously. So yes.

Test:
```rust
#[test]
fn module_load_link_evaluate_checks_cancellation_at_phase_boundaries() {
    let mut context = Context::default();
    let handle = context.new_evaluation_handle();
    let module = Module::parse(Source::from_bytes("globalThis.moduleRan = true;"), None, &mut context).unwrap();
    let promise = module.load_link_evaluate_with_evaluation(&handle, &mut context);
    assert_eq!(promise.state(), PromiseState::Pending); // not yet linked/evaluated

    handle.cancel_with_reason(js_string!("phase reason"));

    context.run_jobs().unwrap();

    assert_eq!(promise.state(), PromiseState::Rejected(JsValue::from(js_string!("phase reason"))));
    // side effect prevented
    assert!(context.eval("globalThis.moduleRan").unwrap().is_undefined());
}
```
Wait - `globalThis.moduleRan = true` is a top-level statement in a module. Does module evaluation set global properties? Module code runs in module
environment, but `globalThis.moduleRan` should still set on global object. So if evaluation ran, it would be true. Since we cancel before
link/evaluate, it should remain undefined. Good.

But there's a subtlety: after `load` resolves and we cancel, the link callback runs during `run_jobs`. But wait - is the `load` promise resolved
immediately, or does the `.then` get scheduled only after a microtask? If `load` resolves synchronously, `.then` enqueues a promise reaction job.
`run_jobs` runs it. Good.

Now, test cancellation during module evaluation:
```rust
#[test]
fn module_evaluate_with_cancellation_during_eval_rejects() {
    let mut context = Context::default();
    let handle = context.new_evaluation_handle();
    let handle_clone = handle.clone();
    context.register_global_callable(js_string!("cancelNow"), 0, NativeFunction::from_copy_closure_with_captures(
        move |_, _, handle, _| { handle.cancel(); Ok(JsValue::undefined()) },
        handle_clone,
    )).unwrap();
    let module = Module::parse(Source::from_bytes("globalThis.before = 1; cancelNow(); globalThis.after = 2;"), None, &mut context).unwrap();
    // Need to link first.
    module.link(&mut context).unwrap();
    let result = module.evaluate_with_evaluation(&handle, &mut context);
    let promise = result.unwrap();
    assert_eq!(promise.state(), PromiseState::Rejected(...));
    assert_eq!(context.eval("globalThis.before").unwrap(), 1.into());
    assert!(context.eval("globalThis.after").unwrap().is_undefined());
}
```
Wait - can module code call a global function `cancelNow`? Module code runs in module scope but globals are accessible. `cancelNow()` should work.
Good. But `module.evaluate` returns `Ok(rejected promise)` on evaluation error, so `result.unwrap()` gives rejected promise. Good.

But wait - does `module.evaluate` require `link` to have been called? Yes, we call `module.link(&mut context).unwrap()` first. For a simple module,
link is synchronous. Good.

Actually, the module evaluation error handling: when VM throws cancellation error during module execution, `source.rs::evaluate` rejects the
capability with `err.into_opaque(context)?`. The `err` is `JsError::from_opaque(reason)`. `into_opaque` returns the reason. So the promise rejects
with the reason. If we cancelled without custom reason, the reason is the AbortError object. Let me use a custom reason to assert equality.

use std::cell::Cell;
use std::rc::Rc;

use crate::{
    Context, JsValue, Module, NativeFunction, Script, Source, js_string,
    builtins::promise::PromiseState,
    job::GenericJob,
};

#[test]
fn basic_cancel() {
    let mut context = Context::default();
    let handle = context.new_evaluation_handle();

    assert!(!handle.is_cancelled());
    assert!(handle.cancellation_reason(&mut context).is_none());

    assert!(handle.cancel());
    assert!(handle.is_cancelled());
    assert!(!handle.cancel());
    assert!(!handle.cancel_with_reason(js_string!("second")));

    let reason = handle
        .cancellation_reason(&mut context)
        .expect("cancelled handle must have a reason");
    let str = reason
        .to_string(&mut context)
        .expect("reason should be stringifiable")
        .to_std_string_escaped();
    assert!(str.contains("AbortError"), "unexpected reason string: {str}");
}

#[test]
fn cancel_with_reason_first_wins() {
    let mut context = Context::default();
    let handle = context.new_evaluation_handle();

    assert!(handle.cancel_with_reason(js_string!("custom reason")));
    assert!(!handle.cancel_with_reason(js_string!("other reason")));
    assert!(handle.is_cancelled());

    let reason = handle
        .cancellation_reason(&mut context)
        .expect("cancelled handle must have a reason");
    assert_eq!(reason, JsValue::from(js_string!("custom reason")));
}

#[test]
fn parent_child_cancellation() {
    let mut context = Context::default();
    let parent = context.new_evaluation_handle();
    let child = parent.child();
    let grandchild = child.child();

    // Child cancellation does not cancel the parent.
    assert!(child.cancel_with_reason(js_string!("child reason")));
    assert!(child.is_cancelled());
    assert!(!parent.is_cancelled());
    assert!(grandchild.is_cancelled());

    // The child has its own first effective reason.
    assert_eq!(
        child
            .cancellation_reason(&mut context)
            .expect("child must have a reason"),
        JsValue::from(js_string!("child reason"))
    );
    // The grandchild inherits the child's reason.
    assert_eq!(
        grandchild
            .cancellation_reason(&mut context)
            .expect("grandchild must have a reason"),
        JsValue::from(js_string!("child reason"))
    );
    assert!(parent.cancellation_reason(&mut context).is_none());

    // Parent cancellation cascades to descendants.
    assert!(parent.cancel_with_reason(js_string!("parent reason")));
    assert!(parent.is_cancelled());
    assert!(!child.cancel());
    assert!(!grandchild.cancel_with_reason(js_string!("grandchild reason")));

    // A descendant with its own first effective reason keeps it.
    assert_eq!(
        child
            .cancellation_reason(&mut context)
            .expect("child must have a reason"),
        JsValue::from(js_string!("child reason"))
    );

    // Other descendants inherit the parent reason.
    let sibling = parent.child();
    assert!(sibling.is_cancelled());
    assert_eq!(
        sibling
            .cancellation_reason(&mut context)
            .expect("sibling must have a reason"),
        JsValue::from(js_string!("parent reason"))
    );
}

#[test]
fn eval_with_cancelled_handle_fails_before_user_code() {
    let mut context = Context::default();
    let handle = context.new_evaluation_handle();
    handle.cancel_with_reason(js_string!("cancelled"));

    let result =
        context.eval_with_evaluation(Source::from_bytes("globalThis.sideEffect = 1;"), &handle);
    assert!(result.is_err());

    // User code must not have run.
    assert!(context
        .eval(Source::from_bytes("globalThis.sideEffect"))
        .expect("context should remain usable")
        .is_undefined());
}

#[test]
fn script_evaluate_with_cancelled_handle_fails_before_user_code() {
    let mut context = Context::default();
    let handle = context.new_evaluation_handle();
    handle.cancel();

    let script =
        Script::parse(Source::from_bytes("globalThis.sideEffect = 1;"), None, &mut context)
            .expect("script should parse");
    assert!(script.evaluate_with_evaluation(&handle, &mut context).is_err());
    assert!(context
        .eval(Source::from_bytes("globalThis.sideEffect"))
        .expect("context should remain usable")
        .is_undefined());
}

#[test]
fn cancel_during_script_stops_later_side_effects() {
    let mut context = Context::default();
    let handle = context.new_evaluation_handle();
    let cancel_handle = handle.clone();

    context
        .register_global_callable(
            js_string!("cancelFn"),
            0,
            NativeFunction::from_copy_closure_with_captures(
                move |_, _, handle, _| {
                    handle.cancel();
                    Ok(JsValue::undefined())
                },
                cancel_handle,
            ),
        )
        .expect("should register global callable");

    let result = context.eval_with_evaluation(
        Source::from_bytes(
            "globalThis.before = 1; cancelFn(); globalThis.after = 2;",
        ),
        &handle,
    );
    assert!(result.is_err());

    assert_eq!(
        context
            .eval(Source::from_bytes("globalThis.before"))
            .expect("context should remain usable"),
        JsValue::from(1)
    );
    assert!(context
        .eval(Source::from_bytes("globalThis.after"))
        .expect("context should remain usable")
        .is_undefined());
}

#[test]
fn enqueue_job_with_cancelled_handle_fails_and_does_not_enqueue() {
    let mut context = Context::default();

    handle.cancel();

    let executed = Rc::new(Cell::new(false));
    let executed_clone = executed.clone();
    let job = GenericJob::new(
        move |_| {
            executed_clone.set(true);
            Ok(JsValue::undefined())
        },
        context.realm().clone(),
    )
    .into();

    assert!(context
        .enqueue_job_with_evaluation(job, &handle)
        .is_err());

    context.run_jobs().expect("run jobs should succeed");
    assert!(!executed.get());
}

#[test]
fn jobs_enqueued_with_handle_are_skipped_when_cancelled() {
    let mut context = Context::default();
    let handle = context.new_evaluation_handle();

    let executed = Rc::new(Cell::new(false));
    let executed_clone = executed.clone();
    let job = GenericJob::new(
        move |_| {
            executed_clone.set(true);
            Ok(JsValue::undefined())
        },
        context.realm().clone(),
    )
    .into();

    context
        .enqueue_job_with_evaluation(job, &handle)
        .expect("handle is not cancelled yet");

    handle.cancel();
    context.run_jobs().expect("run jobs should succeed");
    assert!(!executed.get());
}

#[test]
fn jobs_spawned_under_handle_are_associated_and_skipped() {
    let mut context = Context::default();
    let handle = context.new_evaluation_handle();
    let cancel_handle = handle.clone();
    let executed = Rc::new(Cell::new(false));

    let executed_enqueue = executed.clone();
    let executed_cancel = executed.clone();
    context
        .register_global_callable(
            js_string!("enqueueAndCancel"),
            0,
            NativeFunction::from_copy_closure_with_captures(
                move |_, _, handle, context| {
                    let executed = executed_enqueue.clone();
                    let job = GenericJob::new(
                        move |_| {
                            executed.set(true);
                            Ok(JsValue::undefined())
                        },
                        context.realm().clone(),
                    )
                    .into();
                    context.enqueue_job(job);
                    handle.cancel();
                    Ok(JsValue::undefined())
                },
                (cancel_handle, executed_cancel),
            ),
        )
        .expect("should register global callable");

    let result = context.eval_with_evaluation(
        Source::from_bytes("enqueueAndCancel();"),
        &handle,
    );
    assert!(result.is_err());

    context.run_jobs().expect("run jobs should succeed");
    assert!(!executed.get());
}

    let executed_clone = executed.clone();

        context.realm().clone(),

        .enqueue_job_with_evaluation(job, &handle)
        .expect("handle is not cancelled yet");

}

fn module_load_link_evaluate_checks_cancellation_at_phase_boundaries() {

#[test]
fn module_evaluate_cancellation_during_eval_rejects() {
    let mut context = Context::default();
    let handle = context.new_evaluation_handle();
    let cancel_handle = handle.clone();

    context
        .register_global_callable(
            js_string!("cancelNow"),
            0,
            NativeFunction::from_copy_closure_with_captures(
                move |_, _, handle, _| {
                    handle.cancel();
                    Ok(JsValue::undefined())
                },
                cancel_handle,
            ),
        )
        .expect("should register global callable");

    let module = Module::parse(
        Source::from_bytes(
            "globalThis.before = 1; cancelNow(); globalThis.after = 2;",
        ),
        None,
        &mut context,
    )
    .unwrap();
    module.link(&mut context).expect("module should link");

    let promise = module
        .evaluate_with_evaluation(&handle, &mut context)
        .expect("evaluate_with_evaluation should return Ok with a rejected promise");
    assert!(matches!(promise.state(), PromiseState::Rejected(_)));

    assert_eq!(
        context
            .eval(Source::from_bytes("globalThis.before"))
            .expect("context should remain usable"),
        JsValue::from(1)
    );
    assert!(context
        .eval(Source::from_bytes("globalThis.after"))
        .expect("context should remain usable")
        .is_undefined());
}

//! Evaluation cancellation handles.
//!
//! This module implements cooperative cancellation for JavaScript evaluation through
//! parent/child [`EvaluationHandle`]s. Cancellation is first-wins: the first effective
//! cancellation on a handle (or one of its ancestors) determines the cancellation reason,
//! and later attempts cannot replace it.

use boa_gc::{Finalize, Gc, GcRefCell, Trace};

use crate::{
    Context, JsError, JsNativeError, JsValue, js_string,
};

/// A handle to a cooperative evaluation cancellation state.
///
/// Clones of a handle share the same cancellation state. Child handles created with
/// [`EvaluationHandle::child`] inherit cancellation from their ancestors, but cancelling a
/// child does not cancel its ancestors.
#[derive(Clone, Trace, Finalize)]
pub struct EvaluationHandle {
    inner: Gc<EvaluationInner>,
}

impl std::fmt::Debug for EvaluationHandle {
    fn fmt(&self, f: &mut std::fmt::Formatter<'_>) -> std::fmt::Result {
        f.debug_struct("EvaluationHandle")
            .field("cancelled", &self.is_cancelled())
            .finish()
    }
}

impl PartialEq for EvaluationHandle {
    fn eq(&self, other: &Self) -> bool {
        Gc::ptr_eq(&self.inner, &other.inner)
    }
}

impl Eq for EvaluationHandle {}

#[derive(Debug, Trace, Finalize)]
struct EvaluationInner {
    parent: Option<EvaluationHandle>,
    state: GcRefCell<EvaluationState>,
}

#[derive(Debug, Default, Trace, Finalize)]
struct EvaluationState {
    cancelled: bool,
    reason: Option<JsValue>,
}

impl EvaluationHandle {
    /// Creates a new root evaluation handle.
    pub(crate) fn new() -> Self {
        Self {
            inner: Gc::new(EvaluationInner {
                parent: None,
                state: GcRefCell::new(EvaluationState::default()),
            }),
        }
    }

    /// Creates a child evaluation handle.
    ///
    /// Cancelling this handle does not cancel its parent, but cancelling the parent
    /// cascades to this handle.
    #[must_use]
    pub fn child(&self) -> Self {
        Self {
            inner: Gc::new(EvaluationInner {
                parent: Some(self.clone()),
                state: GcRefCell::new(EvaluationState::default()),
            }),
        }
    }

    /// Cancels this evaluation handle without a custom reason.
    ///
    /// Returns `true` if this call performed the first effective cancellation on this
    /// handle or one of its ancestors, and `false` otherwise.
    #[must_use]
    pub fn cancel(&self) -> bool {
        self.cancel_with_opt_reason(None)
    }

    /// Cancels this evaluation handle with a custom reason.
    ///
    /// Returns `true` if this call performed the first effective cancellation on this
    /// handle or one of its ancestors, and `false` otherwise.
    #[must_use]
    pub fn cancel_with_reason(&self, reason: impl Into<JsValue>) -> bool {
        self.cancel_with_opt_reason(Some(reason.into()))
    }

    fn cancel_with_opt_reason(&self, reason: Option<JsValue>) -> bool {
        if self.is_cancelled() {
            return false;
        }

        let mut state = self.inner.state.borrow_mut();
        if state.cancelled {
            return false;
        }

        state.cancelled = true;
        state.reason = reason;
        true
    }

    /// Returns `true` if this handle, or any of its ancestors, has been cancelled.
    #[must_use]
    pub fn is_cancelled(&self) -> bool {
        let mut current = Some(self.clone());
        while let Some(handle) = current {
            if handle.inner.state.borrow().cancelled {
                return true;
            }
            current = handle.inner.parent.clone();
        }
        false
    }

    /// Returns the first effective cancellation reason for this handle.
    ///
    /// If this handle has its own first effective cancellation, its reason is returned.
    /// Otherwise, the inherited cancellation reason from the nearest cancelled ancestor is
    /// returned. If cancellation happened without a custom reason, an error-like value
    /// whose string contains `AbortError` is produced.
    #[must_use]
    pub fn cancellation_reason(&self, context: &mut Context) -> Option<JsValue> {
        let mut current = Some(self.clone());
        while let Some(handle) = current {
            let state = handle.inner.state.borrow();
            if state.cancelled {
                let reason = state.reason.clone();
                return Some(reason.unwrap_or_else(|| make_abort_error(context)));
            }
            current = handle.inner.parent.clone();
        }
        None
    }
}

/// Creates an `AbortError`-like value used as the default cancellation reason.
pub(crate) fn make_abort_error(context: &mut Context) -> JsValue {
    let obj = JsNativeError::error()
        .with_message("signal is aborted without reason")
        .into_opaque(context);
    obj.set(js_string!("name"), js_string!("AbortError"), false, context)
        .ok();
    obj.into()
}

/// Convenience helper to turn a cancelled handle into a `JsError`.
pub(crate) fn cancellation_error(handle: &EvaluationHandle, context: &mut Context) -> JsError {
    let reason = handle
        .cancellation_reason(context)
        .unwrap_or_else(|| make_abort_error(context));
    JsError::from_opaque(reason)
}

Now, let me think about requirement 5 more: "Cancelling during script execution must stop before later side effects and not corrupt future Context
usage." The per-instruction checkpoint aborts at the next instruction boundary. But there's a subtlety: cancellation is often triggered by a native
function that is *inside* an instruction handler. After the native function returns, the instruction completes, then the next loop iteration checks.
So the instruction that triggered cancellation completes. If that instruction was e.g. a `Call` that has a side effect after the call (like storing
the return value), that side effect happens. That's fine - "stop before later side effects" means later statements/instructions.

Now, let me also think about whether the checkpoint should be inside `execute_one` or at the top of the loop. At the top of the loop is fine.

Now, another concern: The `run_async_with_budget` has a `yield_now().await` when budget is 0. If cancellation happens during an async job that yields,
the checkpoint at the top of the loop catches it after resume. Good.

Now, let me also consider: `Context::run_jobs_with_evaluation` currently pushes handle, calls `run_jobs`, pops. But `SimpleJobExecutor::run_jobs`
itself calls `future::block_on(self.run_jobs_async(...))`. If `run_jobs_async` runs a job that calls `context.enqueue_job`, the active handle is on
the Context (pushed by run_jobs_with_evaluation), so auto-association works. Good.

Now, there's a subtle bug: `Context::enqueue_job` auto-associates with `current_evaluation_handle()` which is the *innermost* active handle. If we're
inside a job that was enqueued with handle H (stored in executor) and the job calls `context.enqueue_job`, is H active? Only if
`run_jobs_with_evaluation` pushed it, or if the job is running under an evaluation that pushed it. But jobs enqueued with H via
`enqueue_job_with_evaluation` (not through run_jobs_with_evaluation) do NOT have H pushed onto the Context's active stack when they run. So jobs
spawned by such a job would NOT be auto-associated with H. Is that a problem? Requirement 10: "Jobs spawned by code that is running under an
evaluation handle are automatically associated with that same handle."

Hmm. Consider: host calls `context.enqueue_job_with_evaluation(job, &handle)` where the job closure itself spawns another job via
`context.enqueue_job`. Should the spawned job be associated with `handle`? "Code that is running under an evaluation handle" - is a job enqueued with
a handle "running under" that handle? Arguably yes. But our current design only sets the active handle when the job is run via
`run_jobs_with_evaluation` or during script/module evaluation. A job enqueued with a handle but run via plain `run_jobs` doesn't have the handle
active.

To fully satisfy requirement 10, when the executor runs a job that has an associated handle, it should push that handle onto the Context's active
evaluation stack for the duration of the job, so any nested `enqueue_job` calls get associated. This is important. Let me modify
`SimpleJobExecutor::run_jobs_async` to push the job's handle before calling it and pop after.

For promise jobs:
```rust
let jobs = mem::take(&mut *self.promise_jobs.borrow_mut());
for (job, handle) in jobs {
    if handle.as_ref().is_none_or(|h| !h.is_cancelled()) {
        let result = if let Some(h) = &handle {
            context.borrow_mut().push_evaluation(h.clone());
            let r = job.call(&mut context.borrow_mut());
            context.borrow_mut().pop_evaluation();
            r
        } else {
            job.call(&mut context.borrow_mut())
        };
        if let Err(err) = result { ... }
    }
}
```
This is a bit verbose. Let me create a helper on Context or a local helper. Actually, I can write a helper function:
```rust
fn call_job_with_handle<T>(context: &RefCell<&mut Context>, handle: Option<&EvaluationHandle>, job: impl FnOnce(&mut Context) -> T) -> T {
    if let Some(handle) = handle {
        context.borrow_mut().push_evaluation(handle.clone());
        let result = job(&mut context.borrow_mut());
        context.borrow_mut().pop_evaluation();
        result
    } else {
        job(&mut context.borrow_mut())
    }
}
```
But `context.borrow_mut()` twice - the first borrow is released after push_evaluation returns (temporary), then job gets a new borrow. Good.

Wait, but there's a borrow problem: `push_evaluation(handle.clone())` takes `&mut Context` obtained from `context.borrow_mut()`. The `RefMut`
temporary is dropped at end of the statement, so the borrow is released before `job(...)`. Good.

Actually, I realize there may be an even simpler approach: have `SimpleJobExecutor::run_jobs_async` call a helper. Let me implement it inline.

Also for timeout jobs and async jobs (when starting them), push the handle. For async jobs, once inserted into the group, the future will poll later;
we need the handle active during polling too. But `NativeAsyncJob::call` returns a Future that must be polled with the handle active. Since the future
is polled by `FutureGroup` outside our control, we can't easily push/pop around each poll. Hmm.

Let me think about async jobs. Requirement 12: "started jobs may complete, while later not-yet-started jobs for the cancelled handle are skipped." For
async jobs, when we start them (insert into group), if the handle is cancelled we skip (not start). If not cancelled, we start; during polling, the
future may spawn jobs. To associate those spawned jobs with the handle, we'd need the handle active during each poll. But the `NativeAsyncJob` future
is polled by `FutureGroup`. We could wrap the future to set the handle active during each poll. That's complex but doable with a wrapper future.

However, is this necessary for the required tests? The requirement says "Jobs spawned by code that is running under an evaluation handle are
automatically associated with that same handle." This likely applies to synchronous code (scripts, module phases) and synchronous jobs. The common
case: a PromiseJob (microtask) runs JS code under a handle; it spawns jobs; they should be associated. For async jobs, the "code" is async and might
spawn jobs; but that's an edge case. Given complexity, I'll focus on making promise/generic/timeout jobs push the handle during execution. For async
jobs, I could also wrap, but let me first implement the synchronous ones.

Actually, wait - there's another important case: when `run_jobs` runs a `PromiseJob` that was enqueued with a handle, and that job evaluates a script
(e.g., a promise reaction that calls `context.eval`), the VM checkpoint should use the handle. If we don't push the handle during the job, the
script's cancellation checkpoints won't see the handle. But the job itself is associated with the handle and will be skipped if the handle is
cancelled *before* it starts. However, if the handle is cancelled *during* the job (e.g., by a native callback), the VM should abort. For that to
work, the handle must be active during the job execution. So yes, we should push the handle during promise/generic/timeout job execution.

This is important for requirement 5/12: cancellation during a job's execution should stop it. So I'll implement pushing the handle around job calls.

For async jobs, the handle should also be active during the async execution for full correctness. Let me think if we can wrap. The
`NativeAsyncJob::call` returns `impl Future`. We can create a wrapper future that sets the handle active on each poll:
```rust
struct WithHandle<F> {
    handle: EvaluationHandle,
    inner: F,
}
impl<F: Future<Output = JsResult<JsValue>>> Future for WithHandle<F> {
    type Output = JsResult<JsValue>;
    fn poll(self: Pin<&mut Self>, cx: &mut task::Context) -> Poll<Self::Output> {
        // Need access to Context, but the future doesn't have it.
    }
}
```
Problem: the wrapper needs access to the Context to push/pop, but the Future doesn't hold a Context reference. `NativeAsyncJob::call` takes `context:
&RefCell<&mut Context>` and returns a Future that borrows it. The wrapper could capture a `&RefCell<&mut Context>`. But `FutureGroup` requires `Send`?
Actually `run_jobs_async` is `!Send` (has `#[allow(clippy::future_not_send)]`), so the future can borrow the local `context` reference. Let me check
the type of `job.call(context)` - it returns `impl Future + Unpin` with lifetime tied to `context`. We could wrap:
```rust
let future = job.call(context);
let future = HandleScope { handle, inner: future, context };
```
where `HandleScope` polls inner while pushing handle. But pushing handle on each poll with `RefCell<&mut Context>`... Let me write:
```rust
struct WithEvaluationHandle<'a, F> {
    handle: EvaluationHandle,
    inner: F,
    context: &'a RefCell<&'a mut Context>,
}
impl<'a, F: Future<Output=JsResult<JsValue>> + Unpin> Future for WithEvaluationHandle<'a, F> {
    type Output = JsResult<JsValue>;
    fn poll(self: Pin<&mut Self>, cx: &mut task::Context<'_>) -> Poll<Self::Output> {
        let this = self.get_mut();
        this.context.borrow_mut().push_evaluation(this.handle.clone());
        let result = Pin::new(&mut this.inner).poll(cx);
        this.context.borrow_mut().pop_evaluation();
        result
    }
}
```
But there's a subtlety: `FutureGroup` from `futures_concurrency` may poll multiple futures concurrently (interleaved) within the same thread.
Pushing/pop on each poll around each async job would work if polls don't overlap (single-threaded, they don't). However, if the future completes
(returns Ready), `poll` returns Ready and we pop. Good. But if the future internally spawns a sub-future that is polled multiple times, each poll
pushes/pops. Fine.

But there's a complication: `NativeAsyncJob::call` returns a future that borrows `context` with lifetime `'a`. So `WithEvaluationHandle<'a, F>` would
have lifetime `'a`, and `FutureGroup` requires `'a` to outlive the loop. `context` is a `&RefCell<&mut Context>` parameter that lives for the whole
`run_jobs_async` call. So it should work. But `FutureGroup::insert` requires the future type... Let me check if `FutureGroup` accepts non-Send futures
with lifetimes. The original code does `group.insert(job.call(context))`, so yes.

However, this adds complexity and risk. Let me first implement the simple push/pop for sync jobs, run the tests, and then decide if async wrapping is
needed. The required behavior list doesn't explicitly test async job spawning under a handle. I'll implement sync jobs now.

Let me update `SimpleJobExecutor::run_jobs_async` to push handle around promise/generic/timeout job calls. Let me rewrite the impl with a helper

```rust
fn call_with_handle<T>(&self, context: &RefCell<&mut Context>, handle: Option<&EvaluationHandle>, f: impl FnOnce(&mut Context) -> T) -> T {
    if let Some(handle) = handle {
        context.borrow_mut().push_evaluation(handle.clone());
        let result = f(&mut context.borrow_mut());
        context.borrow_mut().pop_evaluation();
        result
    } else {
        f(&mut context.borrow_mut())
    }
}
```
